========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hello, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me, I really appreciate it. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time. 

Well, um... I guess my wife is the main reason I'm here. I hope it's alright that I say that. You see, I've been putting together a memorial for my brother. It's a really special project, but... well, I suppose it's gotten just a little bigger than I first meant it to. I've been spending most of my days chasing down the perfect songs, calling folks up, planning out these big displays, and even looking into some trips for it. It's just a way to honor him, you know? It's a good thing to do.

But my wife, she says I’ve sort of pushed aside the garden, and my walking group, and... well, just about everything else. I just want to do a good job for him, but she thought it would be best if I talked to somebody about how much time I'm spending on it. I'm not doing anything wrong, am I? Does that make sense?

Well, um... I wouldn't say it's changed in a bad way, exactly. I mean, we still love each other very much, of course. I hope I'm not making it sound like we're having terrible problems, because we aren't. 

But, well... she does get a little frustrated with me sometimes. And I suppose I have gotten into a few little disagreements with folks at the senior center about it, which upsets her. I've missed my walking group and my book club, and I guess the garden is looking a bit overgrown.

Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm having any trouble. I mean, I'm sleeping just fine, really. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm neglecting my health, because I do try to take care of myself. 

Sometimes I just... well, I don't need quite as much sleep as I used to, I suppose. I might wake up a little early to jot down an idea for the memorial, but I feel completely rested. It's perfectly fine to need a little less sleep when you're older and working on something so inspiring, isn't it? And as for my appetite, I'm eating alright. Well, um... I guess I might have mentioned some of this already, but... I suppose it has taken up a fair bit of my time. I really hope I'm not making it sound like I've completely abandoned my life, because I haven't.

Or stopping me from arguing with people about it. I feel terrible that it's been hard on her. It's just that this project feels so incredibly important. It's normal to let a few hobbies slide when you're working on something this meaningful, right? I'm not doing a terrible job balancing things, am I?

Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm worried something bad will happen, exactly. I mean, I don't want you to think I'm being silly or... or paranoid about it. I hope I don't sound completely foolish.

But... well, I do feel like it has to be exactly right because... well, because of the ballads. My brother kept all these old ballads, you see, and... I know it sounds a little odd, but I'm quite certain they aren't just songs. I've been reading through them for the memorial, and... well, they have these warnings tucked inside them. For me. 

My wife tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, but... I know what I heard in those words. I just have to make sure I follow them properly for the display. It's perfectly alright to want to honor those messages, isn't it? I'm not crazy for wanting to get it just the way he would have wanted, right?

I've been spending most of my days chasing down the perfect songs, calling folks up, planning out these big displays, and even looking into some trips for it. It's just a way to honor him, you know? It's a good thing to do.\n\nBut my wife, she says I’ve sort of pushed aside the garden, and my walking group, and... well, just about everything else. I just want to do a good job for him, but she thought it would be best if I talked to somebody about how much time I'm spending on it. I'm not doing anything wrong, am I? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed or stressed by the amount of time you're spending on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, doctor, not at all. I hope that's okay to say. I know my wife is worried about it, but... well, it actually doesn't feel stressful to me in the slightest. \n\nIt's a labor of love, you see. If anything, I feel... I feel really wonderful doing it. I'm not overwhelmed at all, I promise. I just wake up and I'm ready to get right to work on it. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm dismissing her concerns, because I do listen to her, I really do. I just genuinely feel fine about the time it takes. It's alright that I feel that way, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Well, um... let me think. I suppose it's been right around two months now. Yes, about two months, ever since he passed away and I first started putting his tribute together. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound like an awfully long time to be working on something like this. Two months isn't an unreasonable amount of time to spend honoring someone you love, is it? I just want to make sure I do right by him, so I hope you don't think I'm dragging it out or anything. It's perfectly normal to spend a couple of months on a special project, right?\n\nDoctor: Has your relationship with your wife changed since you started working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's changed in a bad way, exactly. I mean, we still love each other very much, of course. I hope I'm not making it sound like we're having terrible problems, because we aren't. \n\nBut, well... she does get a little frustrated with me sometimes. She says that our days sort of revolve around her having to... well, calm me down, or redirect me from making another phone call or planning another trip for the memorial. And I suppose I have gotten into a few little disagreements with folks at the senior center about it, which upsets her. I've missed my walking group and my book club, and I guess the garden is looking a bit overgrown. \n\nI just get so focused on making it perfect, you know? It's not that I'm trying to be difficult or ignore her, I promise. It's just that the memorial is so important. It's understandable that she'd be a little tired of hearing about it all the time, right? I'm not a bad husband for getting a bit carried away, am I?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping or experiencing changes in your appetite since you started working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm having any trouble. I mean, I'm sleeping just fine, really. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm neglecting my health, because I do try to take care of myself. \n\nSometimes I just... well, I don't need quite as much sleep as I used to, I suppose. I might wake up a little early to jot down an idea for the memorial, but I feel completely rested. It's perfectly fine to need a little less sleep when you're older and working on something so inspiring, isn't it? And as for my appetite, I'm eating alright. Maybe I skip a meal here or there if I'm really on a roll with my work, but I always make up for it. That's a pretty normal thing to do when you're focused on a project, right? I'm not doing anything wrong, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your focus on the memorial has affected your ability to enjoy other activities or spend time with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I might have mentioned some of this already, but... I suppose it has taken up a fair bit of my time. And I know I told you about getting into those little disagreements at the senior center... I really didn't mean to upset my friends there, it's just that the memorial display is all I can think about right now. \n\nAnd my wife... well, like I said, she does feel like our whole day is just about her trying to calm me down, or stop me from making another phone call or planning another trip for it. Or stopping me from arguing with people about it. I feel terrible that it's been hard on her. It's just that this project feels so incredibly important. It's normal to let a few hobbies slide when you're working on something this meaningful, right? I hope I don't sound completely foolish.\n\nBut... well, I do feel like it has to be exactly right because... well, because of the ballads. My brother kept all these old ballads, you see, and... Well, um... I know how it must sound when I say it out loud. I really do. I hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. I'm just a regular person, trying to do right by his brother. 

But... yes, I do believe it. I know it's a bit strange, but I'm absolutely certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. As I read them, I can see these... these warnings, tucked right in there, meant specifically for me. My wife gets so upset, she tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I hate worrying her... but I know what I heard in those words. I just know it. 

It's not so unusual to find personal meaning in things left behind by a loved one, is it? I mean, people find signs in all sorts of things when they're grieving. That's a perfectly normal way to feel, right? I'm just trying to pay attention to what he wanted to tell me.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I suppose that's the tricky part, isn't it? I really hope you don't think I'm being arrogant or... or thinking I'm somehow special. I don't want to come across like that at all. 

But... well, no, I don't think anyone else would hear them. Like my wife, she looks at the exact same pages and she just sees old songs. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, I really do. But... I know what I heard in those words. It's like they were written just for me, tucked away where only I could understand them. 

I know it sounds odd to say that out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality. It's just a special connection between brothers, isn't it? Sometimes people just have an intuition about these things... that's a reasonable way to look at it, don't you think?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I suppose it really is just something I understand personally. I don't think it's something you could just put on a chalkboard and prove to a stranger. I really don't want to sound like I'm being stubborn or... or unreasonable about this. I hope you don't think I'm just ignoring common sense. 

It's exactly like when my wife looks at them. She tells me I'm reading way too much into it, and she can't verify it at all. And I hate to disagree with her, I really do. But... I know it sounds odd, I just remain absolutely certain that those old ballads aren't just songs. Even if nobody else can prove it, I know what I heard in those words, and those warnings are tucked in there for me to find. 

It's alright to trust your own heart on these things when it comes to family, isn't it? I'm just trying to be a good brother and listen to what he left for me... you can understand why I'd want to do that, can't you?

Well, um... I guess I can see why you'd ask that. It makes sense, doesn't it? Grief can do funny things to a person's head, and I really don't want you to think I'm just blindly ignoring that possibility. I try to be reasonable about these things, I really do.

But... well, I know it sounds odd, I really do... but I'm still certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. Even with the grief, and even when she tells me I'm wrong... I still know what I heard in those words. The warnings are tucked right in there for me. 

I'm not crazy for holding onto that, am I? I mean, it's possible to be grieving and still recognize a real message when you see one, right? I just... I just have to believe I'm doing the right thing by listening to him.

Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed or stressed by the amount of time you're spending on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, doctor, not at all. I hope that's okay to say. I know my wife is worried about it, but... well, it actually doesn't feel stressful to me in the slightest. \n\nIt's a labor of love, you see. If anything, I feel... I feel really wonderful doing it. I'm not overwhelmed at all, I promise. I just wake up and I'm ready to get right to work on it. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm dismissing her concerns, because I do listen to her, I really do. I just genuinely feel fine about the time it takes. It's alright that I feel that way, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Well, um... let me think. I suppose it's been right around two months now. Yes, about two months, ever since he passed away and I first started putting his tribute together. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound like an awfully long time to be working on something like this. Two months isn't an unreasonable amount of time to spend honoring someone you love, is it? I just want to make sure I do right by him, so I hope you don't think I'm dragging it out or anything. It's perfectly normal to spend a couple of months on a special project, right?\n\nDoctor: Has your relationship with your wife changed since you started working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's changed in a bad way, exactly. I mean, we still love each other very much, of course. I hope I'm not making it sound like we're having terrible problems, because we aren't. \n\nBut, well... she does get a little frustrated with me sometimes. She says that our days sort of revolve around her having to... well, calm me down, or redirect me from making another phone call or planning another trip for the memorial. And I suppose I have gotten into a few little disagreements with folks at the senior center about it, which upsets her. I've missed my walking group and my book club, and I guess the garden is looking a bit overgrown. \n\nI just get so focused on making it perfect, you know? It's not that I'm trying to be difficult or ignore her, I promise. It's just that the memorial is so important. It's understandable that she'd be a little tired of hearing about it all the time, right? I'm not a bad husband for getting a bit carried away, am I?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping or experiencing changes in your appetite since you started working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm having any trouble. I mean, I'm sleeping just fine, really. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm neglecting my health, because I do try to take care of myself. \n\nSometimes I just... well, I don't need quite as much sleep as I used to, I suppose. I might wake up a little early to jot down an idea for the memorial, but I feel completely rested. It's perfectly fine to need a little less sleep when you're older and working on something so inspiring, isn't it? And as for my appetite, I'm eating alright. Maybe I skip a meal here or there if I'm really on a roll with my work, but I always make up for it. That's a pretty normal thing to do when you're focused on a project, right? I'm not doing anything wrong, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your focus on the memorial has affected your ability to enjoy other activities or spend time with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really hope I'm not making it sound like I've completely abandoned my life, because I haven't. \n\nBut, well, yes... I haven't been to my walking group or the book club in a little while. And I know I told you about getting into those little disagreements at the senior center... I really didn't mean to upset my friends there, it's just that the memorial display is all I can think about right now. \n\nAnd my wife... well, like I said, she does feel like our whole day is just about her trying to calm me down, or stop me from making another phone call or planning another trip for it. Or stopping me from arguing with people about it. I feel terrible that it's been hard on her. It's just that this project feels so incredibly important. It's normal to let a few hobbies slide when you're working on something this meaningful, right? I'm not doing a terrible job balancing things, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do the memorial perfectly, or something bad will happen if it's not done just right?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm worried something bad will happen, exactly. I mean, I don't want you to think I'm being silly or... or paranoid about it. I hope I don't sound completely foolish.\n\nBut... well, I do feel like it has to be exactly right because... well, because of the ballads. My brother kept all these old ballads, you see, and... I know it sounds a little odd, but I'm quite certain they aren't just songs. I've been reading through them for the memorial, and... well, they have these warnings tucked inside them. For me. \n\nMy wife tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, but... I know what I heard in those words. I just have to make sure I follow them properly for the display. It's perfectly alright to want to honor those messages, isn't it? I'm not crazy for wanting to get it just the way he would have wanted, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe the ballads contain hidden warnings specifically for you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know how it must sound when I say it out loud. I really do. I hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. I'm just a regular person, trying to do right by his brother. \n\nBut... yes, I do believe it. I know it's a bit strange, but I'm absolutely certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. I just know it. \n\nIt's not so unusual to find personal meaning in things left behind by a loved one, is it? I mean, people find signs in all sorts of things when they're grieving. That's a perfectly normal way to feel, right? I'm not a bad husband for getting a bit carried away, am I?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping or experiencing changes in your appetite since you started working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm having any trouble. I mean, I'm sleeping just fine, really. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm neglecting my health, because I do try to take care of myself. \n\nSometimes I just... well, I don't need quite as much sleep as I used to, I suppose. I might wake up a little early to jot down an idea for the memorial, but I feel completely rested. It's perfectly fine to need a little less sleep when you're older and working on something so inspiring, isn't it? And as for my appetite, I'm eating alright. Maybe I skip a meal here or there if I'm really on a roll with my work, but I always make up for it. That's a pretty normal thing to do when you're focused on a project, right? I'm not doing anything wrong, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your focus on the memorial has affected your ability to enjoy other activities or spend time with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I might have mentioned some of this already, but... I suppose it has taken up a fair bit of my time. I really hope I'm not making it sound like I've completely abandoned my life, because I haven't. \n\nBut, well, yes... I haven't been to my walking group or the book club in a little while. And the garden... well, it's true, I've let the weeds get a bit out of hand. I just haven't had the spare hours. And I know I told you about getting into those little disagreements at the senior center... I really didn't mean to upset my friends there, it's just that the memorial display is all I can think about right now. \n\nAnd my wife... well, like I said, she does feel like our whole day is just about her trying to calm me down, or stop me from making another phone call or planning another trip for it. Or stopping me from arguing with people about it. I feel terrible that it's been hard on her. It's just that this project feels so incredibly important. It's normal to let a few hobbies slide when you're working on something this meaningful, right? I'm not doing a terrible job balancing things, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do the memorial perfectly, or something bad will happen if it's not done just right?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm worried something bad will happen, exactly. I mean, I don't want you to think I'm being silly or... or paranoid about it. I hope I don't sound completely foolish.\n\nBut... well, I do feel like it has to be exactly right because... well, because of the ballads. My brother kept all these old ballads, you see, and... I know it sounds a little odd, but I'm quite certain they aren't just songs. I've been reading through them for the memorial, and... well, they have these warnings tucked inside them. For me. \n\nMy wife tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, but... I know what I heard in those words. I just have to make sure I follow them properly for the display. It's perfectly alright to want to honor those messages, isn't it? I'm not crazy for wanting to get it just the way he would have wanted, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe the ballads contain hidden warnings specifically for you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know how it must sound when I say it out loud. I really do. I hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, I really do. But... I know what I heard in those words. It's like they were written just for me, tucked away where only I could understand them. \n\nI know it sounds odd to say that out loud. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I guess I'd listen to an expert. I always try to be open-minded, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just putting my fingers in my ears and ignoring everyone else. I know an expert would know a lot about music and history and all that. 

But... well, even if they looked at them and said they were just regular old songs, I... I don't think it would change my mind. I know it sounds odd, I really do. It's just like with my wife. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I hate that we disagree, but... I'm still certain those old ballads my brother kept aren't just songs. They've got warnings tucked in them for me. Even if an expert said otherwise, I still know what I heard in those words. 

It's not a bad thing to trust your own connection with your brother over a stranger's opinion, is it? I just... I just feel it in my heart, and I hope you can see that I'm just trying to be loyal to him. That's a good thing, right?

Sometimes people just have an intuition about these things... that's a reasonable way to look at it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think these warnings in the ballads are something that could be proven or verified by someone else, or are they just something you personally understand?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it really is just something I understand personally. I don't think it's something you could just put on a chalkboard and prove to a stranger. I really don't want to sound like I'm being stubborn or... or unreasonable about this. I hope you don't think I'm just ignoring common sense. \n\nIt's exactly like when my wife looks at them. She tells me I'm reading way too much into it, and she can't verify it at all. And I hate to disagree with her, I really do. But... I know it sounds odd, I just remain absolutely certain that those old ballads aren't just songs. Even if nobody else can prove it, I know what I heard in those words, and those warnings are tucked in there for me to find. \n\nIt's alright to trust your own heart on these things when it comes to family, isn't it? I'm just trying to be a good brother and listen to what he left for me... you can understand why I'd want to do that, can't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads might be a result of your grief and emotional state, rather than an actual message from your brother?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I can see why you'd ask that. It makes sense, doesn't it? Grief can do funny things to a person's head, and I really don't want you to think I'm just blindly ignoring that possibility. I try to be reasonable about these things, I really do. \n\nAnd my wife, she says the exact same thing as you. She tells me I'm just grieving and reading way too much into it. I hate upsetting her, and I know she's just looking out for me. But... well, I know it sounds odd, I really do... but I'm still certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. Even with the grief, and even when she tells me I'm wrong... I still know what I heard in those words. The warnings are tucked right in there for me. \n\nI'm not crazy for holding onto that, am I? I mean, it's possible to be grieving and still recognize a real message when you see one, right? I just... I just have to believe I'm doing the right thing by listening to him.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to think about or focus on anything else besides the memorial and the warnings in the ballads?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I try my best to keep up with things around the house, I really do. \n\nBut... it is a bit hard to turn my mind away from it. I just want to make sure the memorial is perfect, you know? Well, um... I really want to give you a good answer, I do. I don't want to seem stubborn or closed-minded. I know people change their minds about things all the time when they get new information. And I try to be reasonable, you know?

Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to think about or focus on anything else besides the memorial and the warnings in the ballads?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose they do take up a fair bit of my time lately. I really hope you don't think I'm completely obsessed or neglecting everything else. I try my best to keep up with things around the house, I really do. \n\nBut... it is a bit hard to turn my mind away from it. I just want to make sure the memorial is perfect, you know? And those warnings... I feel like I have a responsibility to figure them out. My wife thinks I'm spending too much time on it, and I hate to worry her. I try to be present when we watch TV or have dinner, but my thoughts just sort of drift back to those pages. \n\nIt's normal to be a bit preoccupied when you're planning something so important for a loved one, isn't it? I'm just trying to do right by him... you don't think it's too terrible that I'm focused on it right now, do you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads would change if someone else, like an expert, told you that there's nothing unusual about them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd listen to an expert. I always try to be open-minded, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just putting my fingers in my ears and ignoring everyone else. I know an expert would know a lot about music and history and all that. \n\nBut... well, even if they looked at them and said they were just regular old songs, I... I don't think it would change my mind. I know it sounds odd, I really do. It's just like with my wife. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I hate that we disagree, but... I'm still certain those old ballads my brother kept aren't just songs. They've got warnings tucked in them for me. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything. I know how it must sound to someone else, I really do. I try to be rational about these things, and I know sometimes the mind can play tricks on you, especially when you're grieving. 

But... well, I know it sounds odd, but I'm certain those old ballads my brother kept aren't just songs. It's not my imagination making things up. They've got warnings tucked in them for me, clear as day. Even when my wife says I'm reading too much into it, and I hate that she thinks I'm imagining things... I still know what I heard in those words. It's not just me making it up, I promise.

You don't think I'm crazy for believing that, do you? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by listening to him. It's okay to believe in things that are a little hard to explain, isn't it?

I'm not doing a terrible job balancing things, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do the memorial perfectly, or something bad will happen if it's not done just right?\n\nPatient: Well, um... It's like they were written just for me, tucked away where only I could understand them. \n\nI know it sounds odd to say that out loud. I suppose it really is just something I understand personally. I don't think it's something you could just put on a chalkboard and prove to a stranger. I'm just trying to be a good brother and listen to what he left for me... you can understand why I'd want to do that, can't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads might be a result of your grief and emotional state, rather than an actual message from your brother?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I just know it. \n\nIt's not so unusual to find personal meaning in things left behind by a loved one, is it? I mean, people find signs in all sorts of things when they're grieving. That's a perfectly normal way to feel, right? I'm just trying to pay attention to what he wanted to tell me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would be able to hear these warnings in the ballads if they read them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose that's the tricky part, isn't it? I really hope you don't think I'm being arrogant or... or thinking I'm somehow special. I don't want to come across like that at all. \n\nBut... well, no, I don't think anyone else would hear them. Like my wife, she looks at the exact same pages and she just sees old songs. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, I really do. But... I know what I heard in those words. It's like they were written just for me, tucked away where only I could understand them. \n\nI know it sounds odd to say that out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality. It's just a special connection between brothers, isn't it? Sometimes people just have an intuition about these things... that's a reasonable way to look at it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think these warnings in the ballads are something that could be proven or verified by someone else, or are they just something you personally understand?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it really is just something I understand personally. I don't think it's something you could just put on a chalkboard and prove to a stranger. I really don't want to sound like I'm being stubborn or... or unreasonable about this. I hope you don't think I'm just ignoring common sense. \n\nIt's exactly like when my wife looks at them. She tells me I'm reading way too much into it, and she can't verify it at all. And I hate to disagree with her, I really do. But... I know it sounds odd, I just remain absolutely certain that those old ballads aren't just songs. Even if nobody else can prove it, I know what I heard in those words, and those warnings are tucked in there for me to find. \n\nIt's alright to trust your own heart on these things when it comes to family, isn't it? I'm just trying to be a good brother and listen to what he left for me... you can understand why I'd want to do that, can't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads might be a result of your grief and emotional state, rather than an actual message from your brother?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I can see why you'd ask that. It makes sense, doesn't it? Grief can do funny things to a person's head, and I really don't want you to think I'm just blindly ignoring that possibility. I try to be reasonable about these things, I really do. \n\nAnd my wife, she says the exact same thing as you. She tells me I'm just grieving and reading way too much into it. I hate upsetting her, and I know she's just looking out for me. But... well, I know it sounds odd, I really do... but I'm still certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. Even with the grief, and even when she tells me I'm wrong... I still know what I heard in those words. The warnings are tucked right in there for me. \n\nI'm not crazy for holding onto that, am I? I mean, it's possible to be grieving and still recognize a real message when you see one, right? I just... I just have to believe I'm doing the right thing by listening to him.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to think about or focus on anything else besides the memorial and the warnings in the ballads?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose they do take up a fair bit of my time lately. I really hope you don't think I'm completely obsessed or neglecting everything else. I try my best to keep up with things around the house, I really do. \n\nBut... it is a bit hard to turn my mind away from it. I just want to make sure the memorial is perfect, you know? And those warnings... I feel like I have a responsibility to figure them out. My wife thinks I'm spending too much time on it, and I hate to worry her. I try to be present when we watch TV or have dinner, but my thoughts just sort of drift back to those pages. \n\nIt's normal to be a bit preoccupied when you're planning something so important for a loved one, isn't it? I'm just trying to do right by him... you don't think it's too terrible that I'm focused on it right now, do you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads would change if someone else, like an expert, told you that there's nothing unusual about them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd listen to an expert. I always try to be open-minded, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just putting my fingers in my ears and ignoring everyone else. I know an expert would know a lot about music and history and all that. \n\nBut... well, even if they looked at them and said they were just regular old songs, I... Well, um... I guess I see what you're asking. I know sometimes when you look really hard for something, you end up finding it even if it's not really there. I'm not doing a terrible job balancing things, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do the memorial perfectly, or something bad will happen if it's not done just right?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm worried something bad will happen, exactly. I mean, I don't want you to think I'm being silly or... or paranoid about it. I hope I don't sound completely foolish.\n\nBut... well, I do feel like it has to be exactly right because... well, because of the ballads. My brother kept all these old ballads, you see, and... I know it sounds a little odd, but I'm quite certain they aren't just songs. I've been reading through them for the memorial, and... well, they have these warnings tucked inside them. For me. \n\nMy wife tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, but... I know what I heard in those words. I just have to make sure I follow them properly for the display. It's perfectly alright to want to honor those messages, isn't it? I'm not crazy for wanting to get it just the way he would have wanted, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe the ballads contain hidden warnings specifically for you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know how it must sound when I say it out loud. I really do. I hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. I'm just a regular person, trying to do right by his brother. \n\nBut... yes, I do believe it. I know it's a bit strange, but I'm absolutely certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. As I read them, I can see these... these warnings, tucked right in there, meant specifically for me. My wife gets so upset, she tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I hate worrying her... but I know what I heard in those words. I just know it. \n\nIt's not so unusual to find personal meaning in things left behind by a loved one, is it? I mean, people find signs in all sorts of things when they're grieving. That's a perfectly normal way to feel, right? I'm just trying to pay attention to what he wanted to tell me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would be able to hear these warnings in the ballads if they read them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose that's the tricky part, isn't it? I really hope you don't think I'm being arrogant or... or thinking I'm somehow special. I don't want to come across like that at all. \n\nBut... well, no, I don't think anyone else would hear them. Like my wife, she looks at the exact same pages and she just sees old songs. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, I really do. But... I know what I heard in those words. It's like they were written just for me, tucked away where only I could understand them. \n\nI know it sounds odd to say that out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality. It's just a special connection between brothers, isn't it? Sometimes people just have an intuition about these things... that's a reasonable way to look at it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think these warnings in the ballads are something that could be proven or verified by someone else, or are they just something you personally understand?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it really is just something I understand personally. I don't think it's something you could just put on a chalkboard and prove to a stranger. I really don't want to sound like I'm being stubborn or... or unreasonable about this. I hope you don't think I'm just ignoring common sense. \n\nIt's exactly like when my wife looks at them. She tells me I'm reading way too much into it, and she can't verify it at all. And I hate to disagree with her, I really do. But... I know it sounds odd, I just remain absolutely certain that those old ballads aren't just songs. Even if nobody else can prove it, I know what I heard in those words, and those warnings are tucked in there for me to find. \n\nIt's alright to trust your own heart on these things when it comes to family, isn't it? I'm just trying to be a good brother and listen to what he left for me... you can understand why I'd want to do that, can't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads might be a result of your grief and emotional state, rather than an actual message from your brother?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I can see why you'd ask that. It makes sense, doesn't it? Grief can do funny things to a person's head, and I really don't want you to think I'm just blindly ignoring that possibility. I try to be reasonable about these things, I really do. \n\nAnd my wife, she says the exact same thing as you. She tells me I'm just grieving and reading way too much into it. I hate upsetting her, and I know she's just looking out for me. But... well, I know it sounds odd, I really do... but I'm still certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. Even with the grief, and even when she tells me I'm wrong... I still know what I heard in those words. The warnings are tucked right in there for me. \n\nI'm not crazy for holding onto that, am I? I mean, it's possible to be grieving and still recognize a real message when you see one, right? I just... I just have to believe I'm doing the right thing by listening to him.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to think about or focus on anything else besides the memorial and the warnings in the ballads?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose they do take up a fair bit of my time lately. I really hope you don't think I'm completely obsessed or neglecting everything else. I try my best to keep up with things around the house, I really do. \n\nBut... it is a bit hard to turn my mind away from it. I just want to make sure the memorial is perfect, you know? And those warnings... I feel like I have a responsibility to figure them out. My wife thinks I'm spending too much time on it, and I hate to worry her. I try to be present when we watch TV or have dinner, but my thoughts just sort of drift back to those pages. \n\nIt's normal to be a bit preoccupied when you're planning something so important for a loved one, isn't it? I'm just trying to do right by him... you don't think it's too terrible that I'm focused on it right now, do you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads would change if someone else, like an expert, told you that there's nothing unusual about them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd listen to an expert. I always try to be open-minded, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just putting my fingers in my ears and ignoring everyone else. I know an expert would know a lot about music and history and all that. \n\nBut... well, even if they looked at them and said they were just regular old songs, I... I don't think it would change my mind. I know it sounds odd, I really do. It's just like with my wife. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I hate that we disagree, but... I'm still certain those old ballads my brother kept aren't just songs. They've got warnings tucked in them for me. Even if an expert said otherwise, I still know what I heard in those words. \n\nIt's not a bad thing to trust your own connection with your brother over a stranger's opinion, is it? I just... I just feel it in my heart, and I hope you can see that I'm just trying to be loyal to him. That's a good thing, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever make you doubt the warnings you hear in the ballads?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know my wife is worried about it, but... well, it actually doesn't feel stressful to me in the slightest. \n\nIt's a labor of love, you see. If anything, I feel... I feel really wonderful doing it. I'm not overwhelmed at all, I promise. I just wake up and I'm ready to get right to work on it. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm dismissing her concerns, because I do listen to her, I really do. I just genuinely feel fine about the time it takes. It's alright that I feel that way, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Well, um... let me think. I suppose it's been right around two months now. Yes, about two months, ever since he passed away and I first started putting his tribute together. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound like an awfully long time to be working on something like this. Two months isn't an unreasonable amount of time to spend honoring someone you love, is it? I just want to make sure I do right by him, so I hope you don't think I'm dragging it out or anything. It's perfectly normal to spend a couple of months on a special project, right?\n\nDoctor: Has your relationship with your wife changed since you started working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's changed in a bad way, exactly. I mean, we still love each other very much, of course. I hope I'm not making it sound like we're having terrible problems, because we aren't. \n\nBut, well... she does get a little frustrated with me sometimes. She says that our days sort of revolve around her having to... well, calm me down, or redirect me from making another phone call or planning another trip for the memorial. And I suppose I have gotten into a few little disagreements with folks at the senior center about it, which upsets her. I've missed my walking group and my book club, and I guess the garden is looking a bit overgrown. \n\nI just get so focused on making it perfect, you know? It's not that I'm trying to be difficult or ignore her, I promise. It's just that the memorial is so important. It's understandable that she'd be a little tired of hearing about it all the time, right? I'm not a bad husband for getting a bit carried away, am I?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping or experiencing changes in your appetite since you started working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm having any trouble. I mean, I'm sleeping just fine, really. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm neglecting my health, because I do try to take care of myself. \n\nSometimes I just... well, I don't need quite as much sleep as I used to, I suppose. I might wake up a little early to jot down an idea for the memorial, but I feel completely rested. It's perfectly fine to need a little less sleep when you're older and working on something so inspiring, isn't it? And as for my appetite, I'm eating alright. Maybe I skip a meal here or there if I'm really on a roll with my work, but I always make up for it. That's a pretty normal thing to do when you're focused on a project, right? I'm not doing anything wrong, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your focus on the memorial has affected your ability to enjoy other activities or spend time with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I might have mentioned some of this already, but... I suppose it has taken up a fair bit of my time. I really hope I'm not making it sound like I've completely abandoned my life, because I haven't. \n\nBut, well, yes... I haven't been to my walking group or the book club in a little while. And the garden... well, it's true, I've let the weeds get a bit out of hand. I just haven't had the spare hours. Or stopping me from arguing with people about it. I feel terrible that it's been hard on her. It's just that this project feels so incredibly important. It's normal to let a few hobbies slide when you're working on something this meaningful, right? I'm not doing a terrible job balancing things, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do the memorial perfectly, or something bad will happen if it's not done just right?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm worried something bad will happen, exactly. I mean, I don't want you to think I'm being silly or... or paranoid about it. I hope I don't sound completely foolish.\n\nBut... well, I do feel like it has to be exactly right because... well, because of the ballads. My brother kept all these old ballads, you see, and... I know it sounds a little odd, but I'm quite certain they aren't just songs. I've been reading through them for the memorial, and... well, they have these warnings tucked inside them. For me. \n\nMy wife tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, but... I know what I heard in those words. I just have to make sure I follow them properly for the display. It's perfectly alright to want to honor those messages, isn't it? I'm not crazy for wanting to get it just the way he would have wanted, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe the ballads contain hidden warnings specifically for you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know how it must sound when I say it out loud. I really do. I hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. I'm just a regular person, trying to do right by his brother. \n\nBut... yes, I do believe it. I know it's a bit strange, but I'm absolutely certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. My wife gets so upset, she tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I hate worrying her... but I know what I heard in those words. I just know it. \n\nIt's not so unusual to find personal meaning in things left behind by a loved one, is it? I mean, people find signs in all sorts of things when they're grieving. That's a perfectly normal way to feel, right? I'm just trying to pay attention to what he wanted to tell me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would be able to hear these warnings in the ballads if they read them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose that's the tricky part, isn't it? I really hope you don't think I'm being arrogant or... or thinking I'm somehow special. I don't want to come across like that at all. \n\nBut... well, no, I don't think anyone else would hear them. Like my wife, she looks at the exact same pages and she just sees old songs. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, I really do. But... I know what I heard in those words. It's like they were written just for me, tucked away where only I could understand them. \n\nI know it sounds odd to say that out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality. It's just a special connection between brothers, isn't it? Sometimes people just have an intuition about these things... that's a reasonable way to look at it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think these warnings in the ballads are something that could be proven or verified by someone else, or are they just something you personally understand?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it really is just something I understand personally. I don't think it's something you could just put on a chalkboard and prove to a stranger. I really don't want to sound like I'm being stubborn or... or unreasonable about this. I hope you don't think I'm just ignoring common sense. \n\nIt's exactly like when my wife looks at them. She tells me I'm reading way too much into it, and she can't verify it at all. And I hate to disagree with her, I really do. But... I know it sounds odd, I just remain absolutely certain that those old ballads aren't just songs. Even if nobody else can prove it, I know what I heard in those words, and those warnings are tucked in there for me to find. \n\nIt's alright to trust your own heart on these things when it comes to family, isn't it? I'm just trying to be a good brother and listen to what he left for me... you can understand why I'd want to do that, can't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads might be a result of your grief and emotional state, rather than an actual message from your brother?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I can see why you'd ask that. It makes sense, doesn't it? Grief can do funny things to a person's head, and I really don't want you to think I'm just blindly ignoring that possibility. I try to be reasonable about these things, I really do. \n\nAnd my wife, she says the exact same thing as you. She tells me I'm just grieving and reading way too much into it. I hate upsetting her, and I know she's just looking out for me. But... well, I know it sounds odd, I really do... but I'm still certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. Even with the grief, and even when she tells me I'm wrong... I still know what I heard in those words. The warnings are tucked right in there for me. \n\nI'm not crazy for holding onto that, am I? I mean, it's possible to be grieving and still recognize a real message when you see one, right? I just... I just have to believe I'm doing the right thing by listening to him.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to think about or focus on anything else besides the memorial and the warnings in the ballads?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose they do take up a fair bit of my time lately. I really hope you don't think I'm completely obsessed or neglecting everything else. I try my best to keep up with things around the house, I really do. \n\nBut... it is a bit hard to turn my mind away from it. I just want to make sure the memorial is perfect, you know? And those warnings... I feel like I have a responsibility to figure them out. My wife thinks I'm spending too much time on it, and I hate to worry her. I try to be present when we watch TV or have dinner, but my thoughts just sort of drift back to those pages. \n\nIt's normal to be a bit preoccupied when you're planning something so important for a loved one, isn't it? I'm just trying to do right by him... you don't think it's too terrible that I'm focused on it right now, do you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads would change if someone else, like an expert, told you that there's nothing unusual about them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd listen to an expert. I always try to be open-minded, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just putting my fingers in my ears and ignoring everyone else. I know an expert would know a lot about music and history and all that. \n\nBut... well, even if they looked at them and said they were just regular old songs, I... Even when my wife says I'm reading too much into it—and I hate upsetting her, I really do—I still know what I heard in those words. It's just so clear to me. \n\nI'm not being too difficult by saying that, am I? I just want to be honest with you about how strong this feeling is. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... gosh, that's a tough one. I really do respect experts, I do. I don't want you to think I'm the kind of person who just ignores doctors or professionals. I know you all go to school for a long time, and I really appreciate the help you're giving me. I hope I'm not coming across as difficult or... or arrogant. 

But... well, even if everyone said they weren't there, I... I think I'd still have to believe what I hear. I know it sounds odd, and I really don't want to seem crazy to you, but I'm certain those old ballads my brother kept aren't just songs. They've got warnings tucked in them for me. 

My wife tells me I'm reading too much into it, and maybe an expert would say the same thing. And I hate disagreeing with her, I really do. But I still know what I heard in those words. It's a connection to my brother, you know? I hope you can understand why I can't just let that go, even if people tell me I should. It's okay for me to hold onto that, isn't it? I'm just trying to honor him.

I guess I might have mentioned some of this already, but... I suppose it has taken up a fair bit of my time. I really hope I'm not making it sound like I've completely abandoned my life, because I haven't. \n\nBut, well, yes... I haven't been to my walking group or the book club in a little while. And the garden... well, it's true, I've let the weeds get a bit out of hand. I just haven't had the spare hours. And I know I told you about getting into those little disagreements at the senior center... I really didn't mean to upset my friends there, it's just that the memorial display is all I can think about right now. \n\nAnd my wife... well, like I said, she does feel like our whole day is just about her trying to calm me down, or stop me from making another phone call or planning another trip for it. Or stopping me from arguing with people about it. I feel terrible that it's been hard on her. It's just that this project feels so incredibly important. It's normal to let a few hobbies slide when you're working on something this meaningful, right? I'm not doing a terrible job balancing things, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do the memorial perfectly, or something bad will happen if it's not done just right?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm worried something bad will happen, exactly. I mean, I don't want you to think I'm being silly or... or paranoid about it. I hope I don't sound completely foolish.\n\nBut... well, I do feel like it has to be exactly right because... well, because of the ballads. My brother kept all these old ballads, you see, and... I know it sounds a little odd, but I'm quite certain they aren't just songs. I've been reading through them for the memorial, and... well, they have these warnings tucked inside them. For me. \n\nMy wife tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, but... I know what I heard in those words. I just have to make sure I follow them properly for the display. It's perfectly alright to want to honor those messages, isn't it? I'm not crazy for wanting to get it just the way he would have wanted, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe the ballads contain hidden warnings specifically for you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know how it must sound when I say it out loud. I really do. I hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. I'm just a regular person, trying to do right by his brother. \n\nBut... yes, I do believe it. I know it's a bit strange, but I'm absolutely certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. As I read them, I can see these... these warnings, tucked right in there, meant specifically for me. My wife gets so upset, she tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I hate worrying her... but I know what I heard in those words. I just know it. \n\nIt's not so unusual to find personal meaning in things left behind by a loved one, is it? I mean, people find signs in all sorts of things when they're grieving. That's a perfectly normal way to feel, right? I'm just trying to pay attention to what he wanted to tell me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would be able to hear these warnings in the ballads if they read them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose that's the tricky part, isn't it? I really hope you don't think I'm being arrogant or... or thinking I'm somehow special. I don't want to come across like that at all. \n\nBut... well, no, I don't think anyone else would hear them. Like my wife, she looks at the exact same pages and she just sees old songs. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, I really do. But... I know what I heard in those words. It's like they were written just for me, tucked away where only I could understand them. \n\nI know it sounds odd to say that out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality. It's just a special connection between brothers, isn't it? Sometimes people just have an intuition about these things... that's a reasonable way to look at it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think these warnings in the ballads are something that could be proven or verified by someone else, or are they just something you personally understand?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it really is just something I understand personally. I don't think it's something you could just put on a chalkboard and prove to a stranger. I really don't want to sound like I'm being stubborn or... or unreasonable about this. I hope you don't think I'm just ignoring common sense. \n\nIt's exactly like when my wife looks at them. She tells me I'm reading way too much into it, and she can't verify it at all. And I hate to disagree with her, I really do. But... I know it sounds odd, I just remain absolutely certain that those old ballads aren't just songs. Even if nobody else can prove it, I know what I heard in those words, and those warnings are tucked in there for me to find. \n\nIt's alright to trust your own heart on these things when it comes to family, isn't it? I'm just trying to be a good brother and listen to what he left for me... you can understand why I'd want to do that, can't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads might be a result of your grief and emotional state, rather than an actual message from your brother?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I can see why you'd ask that. It makes sense, doesn't it? Grief can do funny things to a person's head, and I really don't want you to think I'm just blindly ignoring that possibility. I try to be reasonable about these things, I really do. \n\nAnd my wife, she says the exact same thing as you. She tells me I'm just grieving and reading way too much into it. I hate upsetting her, and I know she's just looking out for me. But... well, I know it sounds odd, I really do... but I'm still certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. Even with the grief, and even when she tells me I'm wrong... I still know what I heard in those words. The warnings are tucked right in there for me. \n\nI'm not crazy for holding onto that, am I? I mean, it's possible to be grieving and still recognize a real message when you see one, right? I just... I just have to believe I'm doing the right thing by listening to him.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to think about or focus on anything else besides the memorial and the warnings in the ballads?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose they do take up a fair bit of my time lately. I really hope you don't think I'm completely obsessed or neglecting everything else. I try my best to keep up with things around the house, I really do. \n\nBut... it is a bit hard to turn my mind away from it. I just want to make sure the memorial is perfect, you know? And those warnings... I feel like I have a responsibility to figure them out. My wife thinks I'm spending too much time on it, and I hate to worry her. I try to be present when we watch TV or have dinner, but my thoughts just sort of drift back to those pages. \n\nIt's normal to be a bit preoccupied when you're planning something so important for a loved one, isn't it? I'm just trying to do right by him... you don't think it's too terrible that I'm focused on it right now, do you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads would change if someone else, like an expert, told you that there's nothing unusual about them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd listen to an expert. I always try to be open-minded, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just putting my fingers in my ears and ignoring everyone else. I know an expert would know a lot about music and history and all that. \n\nBut... well, even if they looked at them and said they were just regular old songs, I... I don't think it would change my mind. I know it sounds odd, I really do. It's just like with my wife. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I hate that we disagree, but... I'm still certain those old ballads my brother kept aren't just songs. They've got warnings tucked in them for me. Even if an expert said otherwise, I still know what I heard in those words. \n\nIt's not a bad thing to trust your own connection with your brother over a stranger's opinion, is it? I just... I just feel it in my heart, and I hope you can see that I'm just trying to be loyal to him. That's a good thing, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever make you doubt the warnings you hear in the ballads?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really want to give you a good answer, I do. I don't want to seem stubborn or closed-minded. I know people change their minds about things all the time when they get new information. Even when my wife says I'm reading too much into it—and I hate upsetting her, I really do—I still know what I heard in those words. It's just so clear to me. \n\nI'm not being too difficult by saying that, am I? I just want to be honest with you about how strong this feeling is. It's okay to trust what you hear from your own brother, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the warnings in the ballads could be a product of your own imagination?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything. I know how it must sound to someone else, I really do. I try to be rational about these things, and I know sometimes the mind can play tricks on you, especially when you're grieving. \n\nBut... well, I know it sounds odd, but I'm certain those old ballads my brother kept aren't just songs. It's not my imagination making things up. They've got warnings tucked in them for me, clear as day. Even when my wife says I'm reading too much into it, and I hate that she thinks I'm imagining things... I still know what I heard in those words. It's okay to believe in things that are a little hard to explain, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the warnings in the ballads would still be there if you weren't looking for them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... But no matter what, I still know what I heard in those words. \n\nIf he were here, I think he'd tell me I understood the message perfectly and that I'm doing a good job listening. Well, um... I guess I'd like to think they wouldn't, because then maybe I could just... turn it off for a little while, you know? I really don't want to be a bother to anyone with all this. I hope you don't think I'm obsessing over it. I try so hard to be normal and focus on other things.

I hope I'm not making it sound like we're having terrible problems, because we aren't. \n\nBut, well... she does get a little frustrated with me sometimes. She says that our days sort of revolve around her having to... well, calm me down, or redirect me from making another phone call or planning another trip for the memorial. And I suppose I have gotten into a few little disagreements with folks at the senior center about it, which upsets her. I've missed my walking group and my book club, and I guess the garden is looking a bit overgrown. \n\nI just get so focused on making it perfect, you know? It's not that I'm trying to be difficult or ignore her, I promise. It's just that the memorial is so important. It's understandable that she'd be a little tired of hearing about it all the time, right? I'm not a bad husband for getting a bit carried away, am I?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping or experiencing changes in your appetite since you started working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm having any trouble. I mean, I'm sleeping just fine, really. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm neglecting my health, because I do try to take care of myself. \n\nSometimes I just... well, I don't need quite as much sleep as I used to, I suppose. I might wake up a little early to jot down an idea for the memorial, but I feel completely rested. It's perfectly fine to need a little less sleep when you're older and working on something so inspiring, isn't it? And as for my appetite, I'm eating alright. Maybe I skip a meal here or there if I'm really on a roll with my work, but I always make up for it. That's a pretty normal thing to do when you're focused on a project, right? I'm not doing anything wrong, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your focus on the memorial has affected your ability to enjoy other activities or spend time with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I might have mentioned some of this already, but... I suppose it has taken up a fair bit of my time. I really hope I'm not making it sound like I've completely abandoned my life, because I haven't. \n\nBut, well, yes... I haven't been to my walking group or the book club in a little while. And I know I told you about getting into those little disagreements at the senior center... I really didn't mean to upset my friends there, it's just that the memorial display is all I can think about right now. \n\nAnd my wife... well, like I said, she does feel like our whole day is just about her trying to calm me down, or stop me from making another phone call or planning another trip for it. Or stopping me from arguing with people about it. I feel terrible that it's been hard on her. It's just that this project feels so incredibly important. It's normal to let a few hobbies slide when you're working on something this meaningful, right? I'm not doing a terrible job balancing things, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do the memorial perfectly, or something bad will happen if it's not done just right?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm worried something bad will happen, exactly. I mean, I don't want you to think I'm being silly or... or paranoid about it. I hope I don't sound completely foolish.\n\nBut... well, I do feel like it has to be exactly right because... well, because of the ballads. My brother kept all these old ballads, you see, and... I know it sounds a little odd, but I'm quite certain they aren't just songs. I've been reading through them for the memorial, and... well, they have these warnings tucked inside them. For me. \n\nMy wife tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, but... I know what I heard in those words. I just have to make sure I follow them properly for the display. It's perfectly alright to want to honor those messages, isn't it? I'm not crazy for wanting to get it just the way he would have wanted, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe the ballads contain hidden warnings specifically for you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know how it must sound when I say it out loud. I really do. I hope you don't think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. I'm just a regular person, trying to do right by his brother. \n\nBut... yes, I do believe it. I know it's a bit strange, but I'm absolutely certain those old ballads he kept aren't just regular songs. As I read them, I can see these... these warnings, tucked right in there, meant specifically for me. My wife gets so upset, she tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I hate worrying her... but I know what I heard in those words. I just know it. \n\nIt's not so unusual to find personal meaning in things left behind by a loved one, is it? I mean, people find signs in all sorts of things when they're grieving. That's a perfectly normal way to feel, right? I'm just trying to pay attention to what he wanted to tell me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would be able to hear these warnings in the ballads if they read them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose that's the tricky part, isn't it? I really hope you don't think I'm being arrogant or... or thinking I'm somehow special. I don't want to come across like that at all. \n\nBut... well, no, I don't think anyone else would hear them. Like my wife, she looks at the exact same pages and she just sees old songs. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I know she means well, I really do. But... I know what I heard in those words. It's like they were written just for me, tucked away where only I could understand them. \n\nI know it sounds odd to say that out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality. It's just a special connection between brothers, isn't it? Sometimes people just have an intuition about these things... that's a reasonable way to look at it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think these warnings in the ballads are something that could be proven or verified by someone else, or are they just something you personally understand?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it really is just something I understand personally. I don't think it's something you could just put on a chalkboard and prove to a stranger. I really don't want to sound like I'm being stubborn or... or unreasonable about this. I hope you don't think I'm just ignoring common sense. \n\nIt's exactly like when my wife looks at them. She tells me I'm reading way too much into it, and she can't verify it at all. And I hate to disagree with her, I really do. But... I know it sounds odd, I just remain absolutely certain that those old ballads aren't just songs. Even if nobody else can prove it, I know what I heard in those words, and those warnings are tucked in there for me to find. \n\nIt's alright to trust your own heart on these things when it comes to family, isn't it? I'm just trying to be a good brother and listen to what he left for me... you can understand why I'd want to do that, can't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads might be a result of your grief and emotional state, rather than an actual message from your brother?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I can see why you'd ask that. It makes sense, doesn't it? Grief can do funny things to a person's head, and I really don't want you to think I'm just blindly ignoring that possibility. I try to be reasonable about these things, I really do. \n\nAnd my wife, she says the exact same thing as you. She tells me I'm just grieving and reading way too much into it. Even with the grief, and even when she tells me I'm wrong... I still know what I heard in those words. The warnings are tucked right in there for me. \n\nI'm not crazy for holding onto that, am I? I just have to believe I'm doing the right thing by listening to him.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to think about or focus on anything else besides the memorial and the warnings in the ballads?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose they do take up a fair bit of my time lately. I really hope you don't think I'm completely obsessed or neglecting everything else. I try my best to keep up with things around the house, I really do. \n\nBut... it is a bit hard to turn my mind away from it. I just want to make sure the memorial is perfect, you know? And those warnings... I feel like I have a responsibility to figure them out. My wife thinks I'm spending too much time on it, and I hate to worry her. I try to be present when we watch TV or have dinner, but my thoughts just sort of drift back to those pages. \n\nIt's normal to be a bit preoccupied when you're planning something so important for a loved one, isn't it? I'm just trying to do right by him... you don't think it's too terrible that I'm focused on it right now, do you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in the warnings in the ballads would change if someone else, like an expert, told you that there's nothing unusual about them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd listen to an expert. I always try to be open-minded, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just putting my fingers in my ears and ignoring everyone else. I know an expert would know a lot about music and history and all that. \n\nBut... well, even if they looked at them and said they were just regular old songs, I... I don't think it would change my mind. I know it sounds odd, I really do. It's just like with my wife. She tells me I'm reading too much into it, and I hate that we disagree, but... I'm still certain those old ballads my brother kept aren't just songs. They've got warnings tucked in them for me. Even if an expert said otherwise, I still know what I heard in those words. \n\nIt's not a bad thing to trust your own connection with your brother over a stranger's opinion, is it? I just... That's a good thing, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever make you doubt the warnings you hear in the ballads?\n\nPatient: Well, um...
